# Local amendments to the V3 Rev 2 design (spec D15)

`Practice Match V3.dc.html` = `Practice Match V3.rev2.dc.html` (pristine, never edited) + the entries below, applied by `frontend/tests/design-amendments.ts` and proved byte-for-byte by `frontend/tests/design-amendments.test.ts`. They retire when a re-issued bundle carries them.

### How to write a row (Task HOUSEKEEPING-B, 2026-09-13)

Two gates read these rows, and both take WORDS, not hints.

**If your entry's `find` takes text an earlier entry's `replace` put there**, say so in the "What
changes" column with the token the gate reads:

* `Consumes A14.2` — this entry edits or rewrites the line A14.2 introduced. A bare mention of the
  id does NOT count: rows name other ids for all sorts of reasons, and 44 of the 68 consumed-line
  pairs were "named" by accident before the token rule landed, one of them stating the wrong
  predecessor outright.
* `Supersedes A24.20` — this entry removes a ruled SENTENCE that entry put in the design. Prose is
  a ruling in the member's own words, so taking one out needs this word, not a citation. The other
  direction, `Superseded by A10.2` on the entry that is being retired, counts too (A10's own row).
* The token goes on YOUR row — the row of the entry whose `find` takes the text. A token on some
  other later entry does not count for you: every consumer declares its own consumption.
* Where a consumed line has SEVERAL takers, any one of them may declare it — four lines here do,
  of two kinds. Byte-identical lines introduced by two entries: A24.35 (households) and A24.36
  (income) both wrote the same `source:` line, so the guard cannot tell which taker took which and
  either token counts; the ANCHORS can tell, so decide it by the `find` each entry carries (A24.46
  declares A24.36, A24.47 declares A24.35). And LONG lines that several later `find`s sit inside:
  A5.6's and A5.7's escaped `data-props` JSON lines are 614 and 528 characters, taken by A5.7 and
  A8.8a, and by A8.8a and A8.8b — nothing ambiguous there, and the declaring taker need not be the
  first. Name the RIGHT id either way.

**If you cite a line** (`V3:2399`), the cited line — or one either side, so a citation may name the
anchor a multi-line edit starts from — must carry a DISTINCTIVE line of what your entry put there:
one with a word token that occurs at most four times in the whole amended design. `</sc-if>`,
`</button>` and `return {` anchor nothing. A range is `V3:<first>–<last>` of your entry's OWN
surviving output, in that order; where a later entry rewrote the tail, the range stops where your
text does and the row says so.

**After inserting, removing or re-sizing ANY entry, run `npm run remap:citations`** (Task
HOUSEKEEPING-C, 2026-09-13). An entry whose `replace` is longer than its `find` moves every design
line below it, and every citation below it with them: A33 shifted 132 rows in one commit, and on
2026-09-13 three merges re-mapped roughly 130 citations apiece BY HAND with throwaway scripts. The
tool re-takes every number against the amended design as it stands, prints what it moved, and
writes only citations this gate accepts. It REFUSES rather than guesses — and the answer to a
refusal is a row in `frontend/tests/citation-pins.json`, which names the line by a neighbouring
distinctive anchor and says WHY, never a hand edit. Four rows are pinned today, all for the same
reason: two entries that wrote the same text, or adjacent lines a later `find` swallowed together,
cannot be told apart by the text alone (A24.4/A25.3, A24.35/A24.47).

"Send it again" has two branches (fix round 1, A-S4.1): it re-posts the sign-up when the visitor got here by signing up and still holds the password, and calls `POST /api/auth/verify/resend` — which needs none, only the session — when it does not and an account is loaded, which is how somebody who reached this card by SIGNING IN as an unverified account arrives. Before that endpoint existed the second case posted an empty password and the uniform 202 answered without issuing anything. |
| A8.5 | 2026-09-08 | account screens composed from the V3 gate card — John, 2026-09-08 ('approved') | The form values, setters and submits for the five new cards, plus `goSignOut` for the answer card's footer link. Each mirrors the design's own `form`/`setEmail`/`setPw`/`signIn` trio, returns the adapter's promise, and renders the SERVER's own message. The client checks only that both fields are present and the two passwords match — before the request, so a mistyped confirmation cannot burn a single-use token; strength is the server's (zxcvbn ≥ 3, HIBP). `TOKEN_INVALID` is `app/api/auth.py`'s own code, and all three consume paths raise the same `TokenInvalid`. A successful reset or accept-invite clears its sub-object along with `gateToken`, so the plaintext password does not outlive the request that set it (fix round 1). |

Pixel-safe — every design fixture carries a non-empty string, so the `||` never fires for them. |

| A13.1 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | `setMarket` becomes a class property beside `setF`, taking `setF`'s own "an event OR a bare value" line so the old contract still holds, and closing the menu on a choice. One implementation, called by the dropdown rows. The `setState` body is the old handler's, verbatim. Beside it, `scrollMarketOption` brings a row into view (`scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest" })`) and `moveMarketHighlight` records the keyboard highlight and calls it — the rows are all in the DOM while the menu is open, so no re-render is waited on; the panel's own callback ref calls it again on mount, which is how OPENING scrolls to a highlight seeded before the rows existed. The row is resolved through the field the component recorded (`this._marketMenuEl.querySelector`), not `document.getElementById`: the id is one this component mints, and `setMarket`'s own trigger lookup two lines up is scoped the same way (final review m5). `setMarket` also returns focus to the trigger after a choice, so neither the mouse path nor the keyboard path drops the user on `<body>` (round 4). |
| A13.2 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | `renderVals()`: `marketMenuOpen`, `toggleMarketMenu`, `marketTriggerLabel`, `marketFieldStyle`, `marketSelectStyle`, `marketCaretStyle`, `marketActiveId`, `marketMenuRef`, `marketPanelRef`, `marketMenuKeys` and the option rows — every one modelled key for key on the Market data card's layer menu (`md.toggleLayerMenu`/`md.layerSelectStyle`/`md.layerMenuCaretStyle`) and the compare menu (`md.compareTriggerLabel`/`md.compareMenuRef`), with the row's highlight taking the row's own hover grey. Each row carries an `optId`, which is what `marketActiveId` names. `Object.keys(MARKETS).indexOf(...)` is clamped with `Math.max(0, …)`: a market MARKETS no longer holds (Seed Listings drops a metro with no listings) gave -1, and `keys[-1]` reached `setMarket` as `undefined`. The orphaned `setMarket:` render key is dropped: the `<select>` was its only reader (dead-code rule, A2.3/A2.5). `marketPanelRef` is the panel's own mount ref — the panel's mount is the first moment its rows exist, so it is where OPENING scrolls the highlighted row into view, the arrow keys having seeded the highlight while the panel was still unrendered; the design's own `md.compareMenuRef` idiom, used because `DCLogic.setState` runs its callback before Vue has rendered (final review m2). `toggleMarketMenu` also clears `giveMenu`: opening one menu closes the others, in every direction (final review m7). |
| A13.3 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | The markup. The 40 px field keeps its own declarations and its search glyph and gains `position: relative` (as the "More filters" wrapper has); the `<select>` becomes the layer menu's trigger and a `role="listbox"` panel of `role="option"` rows, anchored `top: 46px; z-index: 700` like the "More filters" popover in the same toolbar row. The trigger carries `aria-activedescendant` (the only element that holds focus) and `aria-controls` naming the panel's `id`, and each row carries its own `id`, so the keyboard highlight is announceable and the reference resolves. The colour-ramp chip is left out — markets have no ramp, and absent beats faked. The trigger is `role="combobox"` and each row carries `tabindex="-1"` (final review I1): ARIA 1.2 supports `aria-activedescendant` on `combobox` and not on `button`, so the whole attribute chain hung off a role that need not expose it, and a listbox driven by that attribute keeps focus on the element holding it rather than letting Tab walk into rows whose keys are not bound. This is the APG Select-Only Combobox pattern the markup already implemented in every other respect. |
| A13.4 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | `trackMenuDismiss()` and its two teardowns, modelled line for line on `trackWidth` and the `componentWillUnmount` it extends. Escape and outside-click are the two dismissals a normal dropdown has; the design has never had either, and they are added for the one menu the ruling names. |
| A13.5 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | `componentDidMount` arms them, immediately after the `trackWidth()` line it mirrors. |
| A13.6 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | Dead code, under the bundle's own rule (as A2.2–A2.5 deleted the `browseSel` orphans): the `market:` render key goes. Its only reader was `<select value="{{ market }}">`, which A13.3 replaced — the amended template holds no `{{ market }}`, and no module under `frontend/src` reads `v.market`. |
| A13.7 | 2026-09-08 | must fix this drop-down to be an acutal drop-down vs the popup following the same design logic of a normal dropdown | The same rule on the option rows A13.2 builds: `v: m` was the `<option value>` a `role="option"` button does not have. The rows read `label`, `selected`, `go`, `rowStyle`, `tickStyle` and `optId`, and `go` closes over `m` itself. Consumes A13.2 (this entry edits the line A13.2 introduced at this site). |

[] : s.sellerListings))`. Three states, not two. `!== undefined`, not `&& length`: a LOADED empty array is a real answer and must empty the table, which is the whole of controller amendment A-SL17 (John, 2026-09-09: "a real seller with zero listings should see the dashboard shell with no invented/sample listings"). Where the array is not there at all, who is asking decides (A-SL23 (2)): with an adapter, zero rows — a real seller is never shown four invented Austin listings because their load failed — and without one, the design's own fixtures, which is the reference and the Claude Design preview. The APP holds `seller-dash`'s frozen pixels through the SUCCESS path instead: the harness answers `GET /api/seller/listings` with those same four rows, derived from this array by `frontend/tests/design-seller-listings.mjs`. |

The REJECTION arm resets what the success arm sets (A-SL25 (2)): it used to reset nothing, so a refused Edit landed the seller on step 1 still holding the listing they had edited before — its id, its fields and its photographs — and every Continue then patched THAT one. With no adapter the design's own path runs unchanged; a refusal shows the server's message in `wizErr`, the design's own error slot. |

Each tile's name is the seller's own caption (A-SL20), then the DESIGN's own slot caption at that position, then "Photo N" past the six slots the design captions — `main`'s A15.3b's own last resort. One chained promise with ONE rejection handler (A-SL23 (4)): choose, upload or store, describe, re-read, and a refusal at any of them lands in `wizErr` rather than escaping unhandled with the new photograph missing from the tiles. A document is routed to the document route by its own type and never asked for a caption (A-SL23 (6) m7). The two guards are separate (A-SL25 (8)): with an adapter the design's counter arm is unreachable, and with no listing behind the wizard the press does nothing at all. | A21.1c | 2026-09-10 | a missing figure is omitted, never zeroed (D-C31, Task B10) | Five script expressions in the `communities()` return, the ROOT CAUSE A21.1 left standing. `num()` returns 0 for null and `parseFloat(...) || 0` did the same for growth, so `pop`, `hh`, `income`, `growth` and the `pets` estimate derived from `hh` were all 0 for a listing we have no figures for — and every `!== undefined` guard added for this task was satisfied by that 0. The panel read "0" Population, "0.0% (5 yrs)", "$0K" Median Income and a "Flat" growth verdict. Each expression now yields `undefined` when its source is null and keeps exactly the parse it had when the source is present: `const hh = p.hh != null ? num(p.hh) : undefined;`, `pop: p.pop != null ? num(p.pop) : undefined`, `income: p.income != null ? num(p.income) : undefined`, `growth: p.growth != null ? (parseFloat(...) || 0) : undefined`, `pets: hh !== undefined ? Math.round(hh * 0.57) : undefined`. `num()` itself is untouched — it is read elsewhere. Every design fixture carries all four strings, so every approved state keeps its pixels. |

Exactly ONE of the card's two menus escapes the collapse, and it is the one John reported: the layer menu's panel (`frontend/src/App.vue:475-476`) is `position: absolute; left: 16px; top: 118px; z-index: 620` and sits OUTSIDE both of the card's `v-if="v.md?.legendOpen"` templates (`:382-410` and `:413-472`; the card div closes at `:411`), so nothing unmounts it and it floats over the map with no card above it. The comparison listbox never floated: its panel (`:434-435`) is nested inside the second `legendOpen` template in normal flow (`margin-top: 6px`) and has always unmounted with the card. `mdCompareMenu` is cleared all the same, for a weaker and different reason — a menu left open in state reappears already-open when the card is expanded again, which is its own surprise — not because it escapes the card's region. The clear is UNCONDITIONAL: the one `setState` runs on expand exactly as on collapse, so neither menu can come back open in either direction (the four-quadrant characterisation in `frontend/src/logic.test.ts` pins both). | A25.1 | 2026-09-10 | a listing with no coordinates keeps its place in the results and does not get a pin (Task MP1) | Ids A20 and A24 are RESERVED and were skipped, not orphaned: A20 by the image-identifiability plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-09-image-identifiability-protection.md`, Tasks P11-P12) and A24 by the neighbourhood-shading spec (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-neighbourhood-shading-design.md` §9, A24.1-A24.8), both in flight at the time of writing — so the next free id after this family is A26, not A24. One script literal (V3:2615): the pin list. `practices: list.map(…)` → `practices: list.filter((p) => Number.isFinite(p.lat) && Number.isFinite(p.lng)).map(…)`. Root cause: `app/api/listings.py`'s `serialise` nulls `lat`/`lng` whenever `location_disclosed` is false, and `migrations/016_listing.sql` declares that column `NOT NULL DEFAULT false` — the nulls are the DEFAULT, not an edge case — and `MarketMapView.vue`'s `engine.marker([p.lat, p.lng], …)` handed them to Leaflet 1.9.4, whose `toLatLng([null, null])` returns `null` (the array branch is gated on `typeof a[0] !== "object"`, and `typeof null === "object"`), so `_setPos` read `.lat` off a null. ONE listing was enough: the `forEach` has no try/catch, so pin drawing stopped there for every later listing, and `LayerGroup.addLayer` had already stored the marker, so the poisoned layer re-threw from inside Leaflet's own event loop on every zoom pass. `Number.isFinite`, not `!= null`: a `NaN` reaches `toLatLng` intact and breaks one step further on. The rail, the count, the sort and the filters are untouched — the listing is real and only the map skips it. Pixel-safe: every design fixture and all eighteen seeds carry a point. |
| A25.2 | 2026-09-10 | a listing with no coordinates keeps its place in the results and does not get a pin (Task MP1) | One script literal (V3:2599): the drive-time ring's centre. `driveCenter: sel ? [sel.lat, sel.lng] : cfg.center` → the same expression with `Number.isFinite` on both of the selection's coordinates. `MarketMapView.vue` guards on `props.driveCenter` being TRUTHY and `[null, null]` is an array, so it passed the guard and reached `engine.ring`, which is `L.circle` and calls the same `toLatLng`. The fallback is the expression's OWN else-branch, the metro centre; no new centre is invented. |

A community with no centroid cannot be shaded. ONLY the map's list: `comms` itself is untouched, so the Market data strip cards still take their metro medians over that listing's figures and the docked panel still reads its own community — a figure is not a point (A21.2n unchanged). |

| A26.1 | 2026-09-11 | the dropdown "more filters" is correct implementation while everything else on the filter bar is implemented incorrectly and not using the site design, this must be corrected | Four class members in the design's script (V3:2279–2317), beside A13.1's metro machinery. `openFilterMenu` is the family's ONE open path — the toggle and the arrow keys both call it — and therefore the only place the five instances name the four overlay menus John's m7 ruling governs; writing `fMenu` is what closes whichever sibling was open (`Object.assign` semantics), so the invariant INSIDE the family is structural and there is nothing to forget. `setFilter` (V3:2295) is the choice: it calls the design's own `setF`, so the state transition and the 320 ms loading settle are byte-for-byte the `<select>`'s `onChange`, then shuts the panel and returns focus to the trigger the way `setMarket` does — the chosen row unmounts under the pointer, so focus would otherwise land on `<body>`. `scrollFilterOption` and `moveFilterHighlight` are A13.1's two, taking the instance's key as their first argument. |

The five ARRAY LITERALS above it are untouched — every key, option value and approved label is exactly as it was; only the render values change, key for key on A13.2's metro menu (`open`↔`marketMenuOpen`, `toggle`↔`toggleMarketMenu`, `caretStyle`↔`marketCaretStyle`, `triggerLabel`↔`marketTriggerLabel`, `hostRef`↔`marketMenuRef`, `panelRef`↔`marketPanelRef`, `keys`↔`marketMenuKeys`, `rowStyle`/`tickStyle` verbatim). `style` is the `<select>`'s own string with three declarations prefixed — `display: inline-flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px;`, which the "More filters" button in the same row already carries — because a `<button>` lays out a label and a chevron where the user agent used to draw its own arrow; nothing else about the closed box changes. `aria` (V3:4086) is derived from the design's OWN first option, all five of which read "&lt;name&gt;: Any", rather than authoring five new strings: these five have no `<label>` and no `aria-label` today, a `<label>` cannot name a `<button>`, and `screens.ts`'s `layerTrigger` addresses the Market data card's two listbox triggers as the UNLABELLED ones. `fMenuAt` is a rendered highlight guarded BY THE KEY (`open && s.fMenuAt === i`), so a stale index can never paint on a sibling. The `<select>`'s three orphaned render keys go with it under the bundle's own dead-code rule, exactly as A13.6/A13.7 dropped `market:` and `v: m`: `value:` fed `value="{{ fl.value }}"`, `v:` fed `<option value="{{ o.v }}">` and `set:` fed `onChange="{{ fl.set }}"`, and after A26.10 the template holds none of the three. `setF` itself, and its event-or-value line, are untouched and still what `setFilter` calls. |
| A26.3 | 2026-09-11 | the dropdown "more filters" is correct implementation while everything else on the filter bar is implemented incorrectly and not using the site design, this must be corrected | One script literal (V3:4018–4086): the `moreFilters:` `.map()` body — the three dropdowns inside the "More filters" popover, Task F2. A `<select>`'s popup is an OPERATING SYSTEM window and renders above this popover's own `z-index: 700`, so converting only the toolbar five would have put the dark menu he photographed on top of his own exemplar, one click deeper, rather than removed it. Key for key on A26.2, because it IS A26.2's loop with three instances instead of five: one state slot, one open path, one set of closures, no new machinery — and the eight filter keys are disjoint (`est`/`ownership`/`sqft` against `type`/`price`/`revenue`/`doctors`/`building`), so `fMenu` still names exactly one dropdown across BOTH loops. Two things differ, and both are the design's own: `label:` stays, because it is the caption the popover renders AND the trigger's accessible name (a `<label>` does not name a `<button>`, which takes its name from its own contents first), so no `aria:` key is invented as A26.2 had to; and `cur` keeps the `|| "Any"` guard the `<select>`'s `value:` carried, because the design's state literal seeds the five toolbar keys and none of these three. The `<select>`'s three orphaned render keys go with it under the bundle's own dead-code rule, exactly as A13.6/A13.7 and A26.2 dropped theirs: `value:` fed `value="{{ mf.value }}"`, `set:` fed `onChange="{{ mf.set }}"` and the rows' `v:` fed `<option value>`. | A26.10 | 2026-09-11 | the dropdown "more filters" is correct implementation while everything else on the filter bar is implemented incorrectly and not using the site design, this must be corrected | One template literal (V3:412–423): the five toolbar filters' `<select>` becomes A13.3's shape per instance — the layer menu's trigger and its panel, wrapped in the `position: relative` div the "More filters" control in this same row already uses so the panel can anchor beneath the field. A `<select>`'s popup is drawn by the OPERATING SYSTEM, not by the page: on macOS Chromium it is the dark menu in John's screenshots, it ignores every declaration in `fl.style`, and it renders above every in-page `z-index`; no CSS reaches it and only replacing the element does. Each row carries an `id` and the TRIGGER carries `aria-activedescendant`, because the focused element is the only place a screen reader reads it and focus stays on the trigger throughout; the trigger is `role="combobox"` (ARIA 1.2 supports `aria-activedescendant` on `combobox`, not on `button` — A13's final review I1) and the rows carry `tabindex="-1"`, the other half of the same pattern. The panel carries NO width declaration: A13's metro panel is `width: 300px` because its field is `min-width: 300px`, these five are five different widths, `min-width: 100%` appears nowhere in the pristine bundle, and an absolutely positioned box with no width shrink-wraps its widest row — which is always at least the current label, since the label IS one of the rows. Absent beats invented. |
| A26.11 | 2026-09-11 | the dropdown "more filters" is correct implementation while everything else on the filter bar is implemented incorrectly and not using the site design, this must be corrected | One template literal (V3:423–441): the popover's three `<select>`s become A26.10's shape per instance, dropped into the design's own `<label>` without disturbing it — the caption `<span>` is byte-unchanged and the field becomes the layer menu's trigger and its panel, wrapped in the `position: relative` div the popover's own parent already uses so the panel can anchor beneath it. The trigger carries `width: 100%`, which the popover's own "Done" button carries and is therefore the design's own declaration; it is load-bearing rather than decorative, because the `<select>` was a flex item of the column `<label>` and stretched, a button inside the new wrapper is not one and would shrink-wrap, and A26.16's `min-width: 100%` resolves against that wrapper — so a trigger narrower than its wrapper would leave the panel wider than the trigger, the opposite of what John ruled. `aria-label` is `mf.label`, the caption already shown: a `<label>` does not name a `<button>`, so without it the trigger would be named by its current option — and it is also what keeps `screens.ts`'s `layerTrigger` (`button[aria-haspopup="listbox"]:not([aria-label])`) pointing at the Market data card's two. Clicking the caption still reaches the control as it did with the `<select>`: a `<label>`'s activation behaviour forwards to its first labelable descendant and does nothing for a click that lands on an interactive descendant, so an option row's own click is not forwarded. |

| A26.16 | 2026-09-11 | the panel takes the width of the trigger that opened it, so their edges line up | One declaration, on BOTH of this family's listbox panels at once (V3:419, V3:450) — Task F1b, run together with Task F2 so the popover's three are born with the width rather than acquiring it in a third pass. | A27.1 | 2026-09-11 | each tile names where its own number comes from, and a ring median says it is approximate (D-C38) | One script literal: the detail's Median income tile. `sub: "Household, 2023"` -> `sub: p.incomeNote || "Household, 2023"`. Root cause: A21.5b/A21.5c wired the Population and Households sub-lines to `communityLabel` and deliberately took only those two — the ones that said "the community" — so the median was the one area figure on the card whose caption could not follow its own geography. It is also the figure that most needs one: a catchment median is a household-weighted median of the tract medians inside the ring (a weighted median since 2026-09-12, Task INCOME-MEDIAN; a weighted average before) rather than a published Census figure (`app/census/materialize.py`), it can never be suppressed, and `docs/integrations/market-data-api.md` has always asked for the word "approximate" beside it. The API composes the whole sub-line (`income_note`) because the tile has ONE sub-line and it must carry the area and the qualifier together. With no note the design's literal stands byte for byte, which is what keeps `detail` on its frozen hash. |
| A27.2 | 2026-09-11 | growth keeps its city-or-county figure and its own sub-line says so (D-C38) | One script literal: the detail's Growth tile sub-line. CHAINED on A21.3d (the `find` is A21.3d's whole `replace`, as A21.5c chains on A12.7's), which already took the vintage from the API's own string. `sub` now reads `p.growthScope + " · since " + year` where the API named a geography, and A21.3d's own `"Since " + year` — or `""` — where it did not. Root cause, and the reason John rejected the cheaper option that relabels every tile uniformly: `population_growth_pct` CANNOT vary by band. `app/census/materialize.py` computes it once per listing outside the band loop and writes that one value into all three bands (plan D12), so a uniform "within about 5 miles" caption would have put the whole City of Dallas under a ring heading — the defect moved one tile over rather than fixed. Its resolution below place-or-county waits on the 2010->2020 tract crosswalk, a registered Phase C deferral: this tile gains an honest label and no neighbourhood detail. Consumes A21.3d (this entry edits the line A21.3d introduced at this site). |

It does NOT route the value through `num()`: that helper strips everything but digits and a dot, so `num(-5.1)` is `5.1` — it would turn a declining area into a growing one, which was invisible while every design community grew and is a first-class value from A24.13 onward. `areaVals(fc, layer)` is the ONE door every polygon enters by, whichever side produced it: the colour is `bucket()`'s and the label is `fmtMetric()`'s, so the fill and the legend cannot disagree, and a value that is absent OR suppressed takes the no-data class while a measured value takes its band even when its margin spans one (D-C36). `areaTip` builds the `rf-tip` HTML ONCE, in the script — it used to be built twice, here and in `MarketMapView.vue`, and kept in step by hand. |

| A24.13 | 2026-09-11 | D-C46 (John, 2026-09-11): the growth class breaks are re-scaled to real ACS data, with a band below zero, so a declining area reads as declining rather than as the bottom of a growth scale | One script literal (V3:1906): `VALUE_LAYERS.growth`'s `buckets` and `stops`. MEASURED, not invented — ACS 5-year place populations, 2014–2018 against 2019–2023, the exact pair `app.census.metrics.population_growth_pct` divides, read from the Census Bureau's keyless summary files for all 50 states, DC and Puerto Rico (29,232 places carry both vintages). The published `[10, 20, 35]` put **79.9 %** of US places of 10,000 people or more into ONE bucket (70.5 % of all US places; 63.1 % of Texas places inside a CBSA), and **30.5 %** of those places were DECLINING with no band to say so — a place that lost population was painted the same colour as one that grew 9 %. The new stops are the tertiles of the non-declining half of that distribution: +3.3 / +8.8 nationally, +4.8 / +13.5 across Texas metro places, +6.6 / +22.6 over all US places, rounded to numbers a 10.5 px legend can carry. On `[0, 5, 15]` the four classes take 30.5 / 32.3 / 25.3 / 11.9 % of US places over 10,000 people. FOUR buckets, not five: the growth ramp has exactly four colours in all three palettes and a fifth class would mean inventing a colour the design does not have, so the below-zero swatch is the ramp's own first colour. It is labelled "Declining" rather than "< 0%" because the ruling is that a declining area READS as declining. `income` and `econ` are untouched: D-C36 froze those bands and this ruling does not reach them. |

| A24.15 | 2026-09-10 | the data is not to the granular level required at neighborhood level — right now it’s just a blob over the whole city and misses the entire point of what is required and the required level of detail and data per neighborhood required to make a decision to buy a practice | One script literal (V3:2170–2181): `loadAreas`, the ONE loader, inserted immediately above `componentDidMount`. Three properties beyond a bare fetch, each because the alternative puts a FALSE map on screen rather than an empty one: it CLEARS `mdAreas` before it asks, so changing metro cannot leave the previous metro's polygons painted over the new metro's view; it ignores an answer for a market the member has since left, because two fetches can resolve out of order and last-write-wins would then strand the wrong metro's boundaries on screen indefinitely rather than transiently; and a refused or empty load EMPTIES the map, never restoring the design's fixture — that fixture is the AUSTIN metro carrying the design's own nine figures, so falling back to it over a real metro would draw the wrong city's outlines under numbers nobody measured. With no adapter the method returns at its first line, which is how the reference and the Claude Design preview keep Task 4's pixels. |

The first was never answerable, so it is not made: with a viewport-publishing adapter AND a metro whose centre is somewhere else, `setMarket` sets `mdAreas: null` — the PENDING state A24.41 keeps the legend's ramp and geography line for, so nothing flickers — and A24.22's listener asks once, with the box the new metro actually settles on. The comparison is the CENTRE and not the name, because what decides whether there is a move to wait for is whether the map is going to move: the same metro re-selected, a metro `MARKETS` no longer holds (`load.ts` prunes it to what the API served, so `MARKETS[v]` can be undefined and no comparison is possible), and every path with no viewport adapter — the reference, the Claude Design preview, any build predating the bbox wiring — keep the design's own immediate load, because there is no listener to wait for and waiting would mean never loading. NOT a guess about which metro a box can see: the boundaries route is bbox-scoped and metro-agnostic (`_BOUNDARY_SQL` filters on level, vintage and `ST_Intersects(geom, bbox)`, never on the CBSA), so shading has always FOLLOWED a member who pans off the selected metro and a client refusing on geometry would blank exactly that. The design's own fixtures switch metro in no approved state, so no frozen hash moves. |

AREA mode (nothing selected) reads the summary's `median` and its five quantiles; LOCATION mode (a practice selected) reads THAT practice's own `comms` row and keeps the metro's distribution as the bars, with the class the practice falls in at full strength and the rest at the caret's own `opacity: .6`. The summary is the adapter's where there is one and `summarySet()`'s where there is not, which is A24.14's ternary exactly. FIVE bars, not the design's seven: five is what the endpoint publishes (p10/p25/p50/p75/p90) and the row is `flex: 1` per bar, so it divides whatever space it has — 38.4 px per bar at the card's 232 px minimum against 26.6 px at seven, and no layout moves. The bars are classed on the MAP's OWN breaks (`bucket`'s third argument, A24.25) because they ARE the map's polygons — measured: a Census tract holds about 1,500 households and the community breaks start at 10,000, so the community scale puts all five quantiles in one class and draws five identical 6 px stubs, the same collapse `AREA_LAYERS` was cut to remove on the map. The LOCATION highlight is drawn ONLY where the practice's figure and the metro's polygons are one measurement — a RATE or a MEDIAN, which is exactly the set `AREA_LAYERS` does not re-scale — because marking a five-mile ring's household COUNT inside a distribution of tract counts would be this ruling's own defect one card over. `stripBasis` goes with the block: in AREA mode the basis is the MAP's own geography, so `metaSource` is asked the same question the legend and the tip ask it (A24.49/A24.50), and in LOCATION mode it is the practice's own label. Consumes A21.2n and A21.2o as well: their metro-median-over-the-defined-values block is replaced by the summary's own median, and their no-figure-no-value contract is kept — `shown` is undefined where there is no figure. | A35.1 | 2026-09-13 | allow a user to zoom in BELOW the level of the last actual map layer — the map allows user to zoom in as far as they want, and this message is never seen (D-C52) | One script literal in `BASEMAPS.map` (`MarketMapV3.jsx:39-43`), the bundle's SECOND amendable file (`file: 'jsx'`, A28.1's precedent). The gray canvas declares `maxNativeZoom: 16` — the last level Esri actually caches it to. Root cause, MEASURED 2026-09-13: Esri's Canvas/World_Light_Gray_Base and its matching Reference layer publish their cache “from Level 0 … through Level 13” worldwide and “from Level 14 … through Level 16” in North America (each service's own `serviceDescription`), and their tilemap reports ZERO cached tiles at 17+ at all nine US points probed. For a tile past 16 the service answers **HTTP 200** with a constant 2,521-byte grey JPEG reading “Map data not yet available” — a SUCCESSFUL image, which is why `errorTileUrl` and `tileerror`, both of which fire on an `<img>` ERROR, could never have caught it. The design gave both layers `maxZoom: 18` and no `maxNativeZoom`, and the map no ceiling of its own, so Leaflet 1.9.4 derived the map's 18 from the layers (`getMaxZoom` → `_layersMaxZoom`), `_getZoomForUrl` put 17 and 18 in the URL, and the placeholder was drawn under live tract polygons, a tooltip and a pin. The fix is the option Leaflet already has for exactly this: REQUEST at the last real level and UPSCALE past it, so the placeholder can never be asked for. |

Depth is per-area and NOT knowable client-side — Chicago and rural TX stop at 19, Dallas, New York, Atlanta and Denver reach 20, Austin, Sacramento and Orlando reach 21 — so a city with deeper imagery gives up one level of native sharpness in exchange for a guarantee that the placeholder cannot appear anywhere. |

The cap is read off the basemap rather than written as a number, so the two can never disagree and a basemap added later carries its own. `detectRetina` stays unset, deliberately: it adds a zoomOffset WITHOUT touching `maxNativeZoom`, which is z17 requests and the placeholder again by another door. |

MEASURED in real Chromium, which is why it is shaped this way — `setUrl` → `GridLayer.redraw()` moves `_tileZoom` to the new clamp and calls `_updateLevels()` and `_update()`, but NOT `_resetGrid()` (`leaflet-src.js:11330-11341`), which is the only place `_globalTileRange` is recomputed; Leaflet's own path for a tile-zoom change is `_setView`, and that one does call it. The probe that isolates it is the same switch at the design's own zoom 10, where both services clamp to the SAME tile zoom and `redraw()` leaves `_tileZoom` alone — twelve imagery tiles requested. `GridLayer.onAdd` → `_resetView()` → `_setView()` with `_tileZoom` undefined is Leaflet's own full reset, `_resetGrid()` included, and it costs nothing a switch was not paying anyway. Re-opening them needs a NINTH declared prototype prop (A16.11a’s mechanism) and a ruled re-pin of those seven, which is John’s to rule; the next free id after this family is A41. Recorded here as well as in `design-amendments.ts` and CLAUDE.md because this ledger is what a future implementer scans for taken ids — A25.1’s row is the precedent. A new class method beside `reloadListings` (V3:3272–3273), CHAINED on A16.17 (the loader it sits next to and copies; A16.17’s own lines are carried forward unchanged, so nothing of it is consumed). `loadAdmin()` is the ONE place the admin screen’s data is read: guarded on `page.admin` through the `perms` adapter — the generated matrix, never a role list written into the design — and then one `loads.push(...)` per tab, each carrying its own rejection arm so a refusal leaves that tab EMPTY rather than back on the design’s fixture rows (A16.17’s discipline, A17.1’s rule for the render path). A36 (Users), A37 (Requests) and A38 (Data Sources) each add ONE line. It returns `Promise.all(loads)`, so a caller — and a test — can await a settled screen. Fix round 1 (2026-09-13, review Minors 5 and 6): the guard FAILS CLOSED — `!this.props.perms || !allowed("page.admin")`, where it was `perms && !allowed(...)` and so asked the permission only when something was there to ask, loading the queue for whoever was signed in on a host with `adminListings` and no `perms` (no such host exists, and the API refuses anyway, but the entry’s prose is unconditional and the guard now matches it) — and BOTH arms answer a promise, `Promise.resolve([])` on the refusal, so A40.5’s “a caller can await a settled screen” holds for every account rather than only for one that may open the screen. A17.2’s other term, `me.state === "active"`, is deliberately NOT restated: `can()`’s `effectiveRoles` returns `['applicant']` for every state but `active`, so a suspended admin holds `page.admin` nowhere, and re-stating it would be the second copy of the matrix this entry exists to remove. |

That line carried `(me.roles || []).some((r) => r === "staff" || r === "admin")` — the LAST copy of the permission matrix left in the design, and the reason a matrix change on the server could not reach it. Arrival still loads exactly as it did; it now asks the same question the API asks. |

| A50.1 | 2026-09-15 | the pet-household rate is the AVMA's, dated, current, centralised and shown as a derived estimate (John, 2026-09-15) | The `AREA_LAYERS` derivation comment (V3:1940), CHAINED on A24.25, whose line this rewrites — **Consumes A24.25**. That comment is the only record of how the pets class breaks were cut ("pets the same times 0.57"), so it is false the moment the rate moves, and a break table nobody can re-derive is a guess with a date on it. The BREAKS THEMSELVES DO NOT MOVE and that is measured, not assumed: `geo_metric._pets` walks the same cached tract scan `_households` walks, writes one row per tract and inherits its suppression, so the pets layer is `round(households × rate)` over EXACTLY the rows households serves — a monotone transform, whose positional quantiles are therefore the households quantiles through the rate. `tests/census/test_pet_rate.py::test_the_pets_class_breaks_are_the_households_breaks_through_the_rate` pins that identity using `scripts/measure_area_breaks.py`'s OWN `quantile`, so the re-derivation runs off the measurement of record (QA, 2026-09-12: 83,783 served tracts, p25 1,054, p50 1,446, p75 1,897) instead of a second national load, which this environment cannot do — the Census API has required a key since 2025 and `CENSUS_API_KEY` is worker-only. 0.586 gives 618 / 847 / 1,112 where 0.57 gave 601 / 824 / 1,081, and both round to the `[600, 850, 1100]` already in the table under the rounding the table itself used. `VALUE_LAYERS.pets.stops` is untouched and is NOT derived from the rate: it is the design's own community scale, one round number per class, and `households` beside it reads `[10000, 25000, 45000]`, which is not this table times any rate. |

| A51.1 | 2026-09-16 | add a 'recenter' button that recenters the map on the selected city and or if selected practice location, place this recenter icon between the + and − | John's words, verbatim: "add a 'recenter' button that recenters the map on the selected city and or if selected practice location, place this recenter icon between the + \| −". The ruling column writes that vertical bar as "and" because a markdown cell cannot carry a raw one and `design-amendments.test.ts` reads the column byte for byte; nothing else of his wording is changed. One script literal in `MarketMapV3.jsx` (`MarketMapV3.jsx:283-295`), the bundle's SECOND amendable file (`file: 'jsx'`, A28.1's precedent): `recenterView`, declared above the control stack's own style constants. TWO ARMS, which are the ruling — a selected practice centres the map on ITS OWN point at the map's CURRENT zoom, and nothing selected restores the metro's own centre and zoom. The zoom is untouched on the first arm deliberately (a control that silently zooms performs a second action its label does not name) and restored on the second, because that is what "recenter on the selected city" means. `Number.isFinite`, never `!= null`: `location_disclosed` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (`migrations/016_listing.sql`) so a published listing can be served `lat: null, lng: null`, `md.practices` already drops such a listing (A25.1), and a NaN passes the looser test — a missing point takes the metro centre rather than [0, 0] or a centre the practice is not at. **It writes NO state, and that is the mechanism rather than a side effect**: it drives the map directly (`mapRef.current.setView(...)`) exactly as its two neighbours do and exactly as V2's own Recenter button did (`design_handoff_practice_match_v2/MarketMap.jsx:236`), so the selected practice stays selected, its docked panel stays open, and `md.resetView`/`md.recenterKey` are untouched — the bottom-right "Reset view" button that has always called `resetView` keeps its exact behaviour (clear the selection, return to the metro), and the two controls differ only when a practice is selected, which is the case this request is about. The boundary-request optimisation in `MarketMapView.vue`'s recentre watcher is never reached: a `setView` settles into Leaflet's own `moveend`, which publishes WITHOUT force, so a recenter on an unmoved map asks the API for nothing and a recenter after a pan asks exactly once (proved in `src/components/MarketMapView.test.ts`). |
| A51.2 | 2026-09-16 | add a 'recenter' button that recenters the map on the selected city and or if selected practice location, place this recenter icon between the + and − | One template literal in `MarketMapV3.jsx` (`MarketMapV3.jsx:369-378`): the button itself, plus a SECOND copy of the row's own 1 px `#e6e6e6` divider, inserted after the first so the order is Zoom in, divider, Recenter, divider, Zoom out — "between the + \| −", literally. The stack is `+` and `−` and nothing else since A49 withdrew the desktop mount's `on-basemap` (the Map / Satellite pair sits behind `onBasemap &&`), so there is exactly one flex row to join. NO NEW STYLE TOKEN: the button takes `stackBtn` with the same two overrides its neighbours carry (`Object.assign({}, stackBtn, { flex: 1, width: "auto" })`) and the glyph takes `ctrlIcon` — the file's OWN control-stack icon style (V2's `iconImg` body, `display: block; opacity: 0.85`), declared here since the V3 rewrite and read by nothing until now, so this entry also retires an orphan rather than adding one. THE ICON is the bundle's own `assets/icons/sub-recenter-disc.svg`, a filled disc with four knocked-out crosshair ticks and a centre dot — the glyph in John's screenshot, and V2's own choice for this exact button; `sub-reset-view.svg` was measured against the screenshot and rejected (an arc plus an arrowhead — the "reload" glyph, and already spoken for by the bottom-right "Reset view" button). Seller dashboard must provide a buyer-access area (directive §5). Task 14 of docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-18-per-buyer-disclosure.md: the interest modal, My Requests and the seller inbox read and write the real access-request API instead of the design's own shared requests fixture, on adapter presence and never on data. | The buyer's own "My Requests" list (`renderVals()`'s `reqList`) reads the new `myReqs()` helper (A52.2) instead of `s.requests` directly, once an adapter is present — one literal edit. |

| A58.1 | 2026-09-23 | John, 2026-09-23: "implement full seller wizard audit" (ruling D-C65), on finding S2 of the eight-step audit — the wizard's Submit button told a seller their listing had been submitted when the server had refused it. The handler read `(promise-or-Promise.resolve()) && this.setState({ wizSubmitted: true, ... })`, and a Promise is always truthy, so the "Submitted" card was drawn unconditionally and synchronously — before the API had answered anything. Submit now flips that card only on an answer that accepted it: with no adapter (the reference, the Claude Design preview) the design's own synchronous path is unchanged, with an adapter a refusal leaves the seller on the preview with the refusal in the design's own error slot, and only an acceptance draws the card and reloads the seller's own rows. | Finding S2 of the seller-wizard audit, and the one piece of it that reaches the ported script. The wizard's `submit` handler (V3:3636–3641) becomes a block: a `showSubmitted` thunk carrying the design's own `setState` byte for byte, then `if (!this.props.listings || !s.editingId) return showSubmitted();` — the `next:` handler's own guard, so the reference, the Claude Design preview and a wizard with no draft behind it keep today's synchronous flip and today's optimistic row — and then the adapter's own `submit(...)`, whose FULFILLED arm alone calls `showSubmitted()` and returns `this.reloadListings()`. Root cause: `(promise) && this.setState({ wizSubmitted: true, ... })` — a Promise is always truthy, so the "Submitted" card was drawn before the server answered, the refusal was computed into `wiz.errorText`, which the template renders inside `isPreview` and not inside `isDone`, and on the reference path an optimistic `in_review` row survived in the fixture list because the reload runs only on success — with an adapter A16.1's ternary renders `myListings`, so that row was written to state and drawn nowhere. A thunk rather than a pre-computed object so `this.state.sellerListings` is still read at the moment of the flip. Consumes A16.7. |

THE TWO ARE SEPARATED ON PURPOSE: what is drawn is keyed on the DATA alone and never on adapter presence, because the reference receives no adapter and a control that renders on one target and not the other is a pixel `maxDiffPixels: 0` can never reconcile (A40.1/A40.2's own held lesson); what is CALLED is keyed on `s.editingId` exactly as A16.21's `describe` already is, so the reference's own tile carries an inert control precisely as its click target is inert today. "That could not be removed." is the design's own error sentence one verb over ("That could not be saved.", "That file could not be uploaded.", "That could not be submitted."), landing in the design's own `wizErr` slot. Consumes A20.4: this `find` is that entry's whole two-line `replace`, the fallback branch carried forward byte for byte, so the no-adapter path keeps every pixel. |

A blank the seller SAVED is the empty string and still clears the caption.
